Levent Sky Engin Altay Free Special CHP Group Vice President CHP Group Deputy Chair CHP CHP Group Vice President The ongoing pressures for freedom of press and expression in Turkey have been increasing its violence in recent months. Journalists are subject to investigations, detention and even punishment for their work alone, because of the news they write. Journalists are being openly threatened, and those who do not comply are being attacked in the streets. Apart from criticizing the government and the President, even journalists who do not give clear commendation are under heavy pressure. Media bosses fire many employees with public pressure from the government and the President. The newspapers and television buildings are under attack with stones and sticks, and the attackers are not punished. Newspapers, magazines, and television buildings are printed by law enforcement forces, the distribution of printed literature is blocked, TV screens are blacked out. After the seizure by the TMSF, media companies that have been handed over to those who support the power have been transformed into the voice of power by feeding on funds from public companies and the money collected in pools of contractors who have received tenders from public companies. Media organizations have been assigned names close to power as trustees, while independent media companies are threatened with confiscation. The broadcast policies of confiscated newspapers and televisions are changed 180 degrees. Workers who work in these ruling organizations are being forced out of their jobs by exacerbating legal rights and using force. Journalists face the actual obstruction of law enforcement during their duties and are detained for no reason. The power is systematically obstructing the public's right to be informed by its pressure and intimidation policy. The most concrete example of this is that Cumhuriyet daily Editor-in-Chief Can Dündar and Ankara Representative Erdem Gül have been declared guilty and targeted by the irresponsible wing of the administration due to reports on MIT trucks. During the remaining period, dozens of journalists were arrested for journalistic activities, held in prisons. Many journalists have been tried and tried for hundreds of years in prison for reports and comments written by him, with ridiculous allegations such as espionage, membership in a terrorist organization, aiding a terrorist organization. The world recognises Turkey with a bad reputation, such as "the country with the most journalists in prison." The government uses the judiciary and law enforcement force aggressively to destroy freedom of the press with raids, searches, investigations, lawsuits and unlawful decisions. The ban on publication on all major events that have rocked Turkey also undermines public freedom of information. These practices are the basis for the blackout of facts and the abduction of the public. At this point, it is impossible to talk about universal media freedom in Turkey. By using its power with a oppressive understanding, power of power, power of press and expression has effectively eliminated freedom of expression, as well as the separation of forces. There is a process in which the provisions of international contracts, which we are part of, are clearly violated. Systems where there is no freedom of the press are called didictatorship. It has been noted in history that authorities who seek to conceal their crimes against humanity have followed a oppressive attitude towards the press. International trade organisations have made statements that political power has become increasingly distant from international norms and has evolved into authoritarian trends. Free and independent press is the foundation of democracy. To destroy the freedom and independence of the press is to destroy democracy. The government has taken a dangerous path to the future of our democracy. The TBMM has to say, "Stop." For the reasons stated above, it is of great importance that our great Parliament address this matter urgently. Under the stairs is the source of silica, which has been exposed to many workers who work in primitive conditions in the illegal eschewing workshops. While there are many different methods of sanding used in industry for reasons such as bleaching, weariness, dye picking, and booger picking, in our country, sea sand is used with high and cheap silica content. With the loss of several workers due to the disease, the public responses were prohibited from using any substance containing sand (silis dust) or silica crystals in a generic published by the Ministry of Health in April 2009. Despite this limited ban order by the Ministry of Health, digging, drilling, tunneling operations; quarrying and mines (sorts, gold, tin, iron, copper, nickel, silver, granite, tungsten, uranium etc.) It's a risky task force in terms of silicone. The preparation of steel to paint is also part of the risk of building jewelry, processing, polishing, building pencils; making glass, crystal, brick production; cleaning, perdaching, waxing, waxing, dental prosthetics, such as cleaning, crystal, brick production; cleaning from the cast of documents. Workers working in ceramic and marble factories operating in Bilıkk, Bozizık and Willow are also in danger of silicone. It's not the same risk of seeing silicone in every line of work. It increases the risk of the Silika substance being used in closed environments, where the ventilation conditions are bad. It takes about 20 to 30 years for the disease to disrupt the respiratory system and expose clinical symptoms. However, in some cases, ten years is much shorter for workers exposed to dust to develop the disease. In the jean-bolting industry, where heavy dust is used in the closed environment, the disease can be seen very quickly. Silicosis is also considered a sign of development for countries. Due to improved working conditions in developed countries and the complete abandonment of risky labour branches, significant distances have been taken to prevent disease. In most European countries, silica use is strictly prohibited. The sanding process is concentrated on ceramics, tunnels, minerals, etc. It is important to establish a parliamentary research commission in order to determine working conditions in sectors such as this, in line with the relevant legislation and conditions of today, to correct this painting, which cannot be accepted in terms of human health, to prevent the loss of the lives of our citizens, to examine it in all its dimensions. Turkey to the chairman of the Grand National Assembly I respectfully submit to the opening of Parliamental research in accordance with Articles 104 and 105 of the 98th and TBM Institution, in order to determine the measures that should be taken by investigating the problems of our disabled citizens. [[Manisa]] [[Mersin]] [[Gaziantep]] [[Aydın]] [[Mersin]] [[Scholarship]] [[Adana]] [[Balıkesir]] [[Adana]] [[Mughle]] [[Inconceived]] 12) Ismail Faruk Aksu [[İstanbul]] [[Ankara]] [[Osmaniye]] [[Ankara]] [[Isparta]] 17) Mehmet Necmettin Ahrazoğlu [[Hatay]] [[Ankara]] 19) Emin Haluk Ayhan [[Denizli]] [[Samsun]] Turkey's Responsibility Research, conducted by the State Statistical Institute in 2002, has been determined by the numerical status of the disabled in our country in 2002. According to this study, 8 million 431,937 people live their lives as disabled people in our country. The ratio of this to the general population is 12.29 per cent. 36.34 per cent of disabled people are illiterate, 77.81 per cent do not work. During the AKP period, no extensive research has been conducted on disabled persons. Thus, authorities of the Ministry of Family and Social Policy do not know the number of disabled citizens in the field of responsibility, their disability situation, education, health and employment situation. The Ministry of Family and Social Policy, which spend millions on building and car rentals, intends to implement projects with state support to resolve the problems of disabled persons, "There is no resource." Refusal on grounds of rejection. It is not only the duty of the modern government to improve and oversee the rights of disabled individuals. At the same time, the state has to ensure that the disabled develop in line with their abilities and potential, that they have equal opportunities, that they have economic and social well-being. However, about 10 million disabled citizens in Turkey have problems in every area from education to health, employment to transportation. All services related to disabled persons, all concerned institutions and organisations have not been possible until now by co-operation and co-ordination of joint plans and programmes, and by spreading national policies. The fact that many institutions and organisations in our country are not considered as a whole in the field of disability and rehabilitation services, has led to various and disorganized legislation, complexity in services, disconnection, lack of surveillance, and confusion in duty and authority. Social care services and vocational rehabilitation centres for disabled people are extremely inadequate in our country. The rehabilitation services are unable to reach the goal of the same vocational rehabilitation programme for disabled persons from various obstacle groups. The number of schools and teachers providing special education and knitting for disabled persons is extremely inadequate. The state is obliged to create jobs for which all its citizens can earn enough income to sustain their lives. This obligation also applies to disabled persons. In our country, employment policies for disabled people are largely based on quota techniques. According to Labour Law 4857, 50 or more workers must be employed in private sectors for 3 per cent and 4 per cent in public workplaces. According to the 657 State Officials' Law, the rate is 3%. According to the November 2015 data of the State Chief of Staff, there are 63,207 disabled officers in public, 40,656 disabled officers working; therefore there are 22.553 people in the public office. In the private sector, there are 23,000 people in the open-impaired workers' quota. This data shows that the quota system does not contribute to the participation of disabled people in the labour market, employment, work peace and business relations. As part of Labour Law 4857, the employment of disabled workers is authorized and responsible for the employment of Turkey's Employment Institution General Directorate. However, Turkey's Business Agency is unable to carry out its mandate to offer labour force to the market, and it remains employed only as part of its demands. Since there are less than 50 workers employed in rural areas and less developed areas, the disabled in these areas are unable to benefit from the quota system. Employers do not follow this rule because punishments for employers who do not employ disabled workers are not deterrents. One of the most important methods in the employment of disabled persons is protected businesses. A provision has been made regarding protected businesses in Article 14 of Law 6/2/2014 and Law 6518 and Article 5378 of the Law on the disabled. However, regulations are still not issued. The parliamentary research commission will be established in accordance with Articles 104 and 105 of the Constitution's 98th and TBM Institution to investigate the problems of disabled citizens. It has been presented to your knowledge. The motions will be placed on the agenda, and talks on whether or not the parliamentary investigation should be opened will be held at the appropriate time.
